ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u4	Thank you, Madam President. He said that they are general state budgets very positive for our country because they are also aimed at providing stability and certainty for our country at a time like this, when it is most needed. That is why they are solvent and realistic budgets. We give certainty and we give security, for example, to pensioners, to those who are going to raise the pension what goes up life, what goes up the CPI. We also guarantee this by providing the Reserve Fund with EUR 3 billion, for the first time in ten years. This Government not only updates pensions, but also ensures the sustainability of the system with such contributions to the pension shelter. And we also give certainty to young people, we help them with scholarships and with facilities for rent. And we also give security, we give a safety net to the most vulnerable with minimum income or the electric bonus. That's why they're very good state budgets. And also for the middle classes, for example, with the gratuitousness of public transport or with a departure as important as that which is destined for scholarships for Spanish families. Thirdly, these general state budgets are very positive because they protect the social majority and deepen our welfare state. Social investment has no precedent in any general budget of the previous State: six out of every ten euros go to social investment. To give us an idea, it is 35% more than the budgets we find for 2018, when we begin to rule. And, of course, they strengthen public policies as basic as health or dependency policies. In short, they are the budgets that the country needs, lay the foundations for a modern, digital, sustainable and just country and give stability and certainty to citizens because they protect and guarantee them social well-being and personal well-being. That is why I wanted to start by setting this framework, the framework of the general budgets of the State and what they mean to our country. Sometimes we get involved in sterile debates, which are not of interest to the citizenry, but I think that this is precisely the one that interests them most, the one concerning the budgets that make life easier for the whole citizenry at a time of difficulty. Sitting this, I go on to detail to you the fundamental aspects of the budget of section 25, of the ministry that I have the honour to direct. You know that the Ministry of the Presidency, Relations with the Democratic Courts and Memories is an eminently instrumental, non-investor ministry, and includes functions of support both to the Presidency of the Government and to the Head of State. Without including transfers to autonomous bodies - then I will refer to them in the last part of my speech - my ministry's budget is EUR 283 million, an increase of 50% over the current year's budget. And I'll explain to you why there's such a big increase. There are three aspects that justify this: the first is the Spanish Presidency of the Council of the European Union, which is to be held in the second half of next year; the second element is the adoption of the Law on Democratic Memory and the budget headings that are attached to this new norm, and, thirdly, the budgetary effort that we also dedicate to National Heritage as a result of the inauguration during the European Presidency of which it will be a new emblematic museum of Madrid, the Gallery of the Royal Collections. As you know, in the second half of 2023 we will have the European Presidency, Spain will welcome our European Union partners under it. And the planned expenditure is EUR 145 million, let me put it in context, because it is smaller than that of France, which has had the Presidency during the first half of this year and has been spent EUR 148 million - we budget 145-, and it is also smaller than that of the Czech Republic, which has the Presidency of the Union at this time, and it is also smaller than what Sweden has budgeted for its Presidency in the first half of 2023. In relative terms, which will be easier to understand, we are going to allocate three euros per inhabitant to the European Presidency, while the Czech Republic has allocated 8.67 euros per inhabitant and Sweden 11.11 euros per inhabitant. Therefore, of the EUR 145 million that the European Presidency is going to assume, 55% is the responsibility of the Ministry of the Presidency, Relations with the Courts and Democratic Memory, and this explains to a great extent the increase in the budget. In order to get an idea of what the budget of the Ministry of the Presidency is increasing, nine out of every ten euros of the increase has been earmarked for the Presidency of the European Union. This amount will be allocated primarily to the Coordination Office for the Spanish Presidency of the European Union, which will depend on the Office of the Presidency of the Government, and also to all the costs obviously incurred by this Presidency, which are attached to both the Office of the Presidency of the Government and the Secretariat of the State of Communication. Secondly, with regard to the second reason why the Ministry's budget is being increased, we must fulfil the obligations of the recently adopted Democratic Memory Act, which must obviously have a development and a budgetary reflection. The memory item in 2023 amounts to 14 million euros, which means an increase of 17%, quite the opposite of with other previous governments, boasting of leaving that budget item at zero, boasting, by the way, of not complying with the law, which remains a tradition in the People's Party. [[Protests]] Failure to renew the General Council of the Judiciary is a violation of the law and the Constitution which is flagrant, deliberate, rebellious and absolutely obvious. [[Applauds]] And, therefore, I do not think this matter deserves much debate either. In addition, almost half of the budget for democratic memory is earmarked for financing the third annuality of the exhumation plan, EUR 6.35 million, in collaboration with almost all the autonomous communities, local authorities and a very large number of municipalities. The increase in the National Heritage budget, which is increased by 26%, to EUR 148 million, is also noteworthy, as I told you. This decision, this increase is the result of a project which, if I may say so, we are particularly proud of in the government, because we will be able to complete a project dating back almost twenty-five years, since in 1998 the one which, during this European Presidency, will finally be the opening of the Gallery of the Royal Collections, in the spring or next summer. It will be a very important new tourist reference for the city of Madrid, and I say to you that our idea is for the inauguration to take place during the European Presidency. This museum will be projected not only at national level, but also at international level because of the quality of the heritage that will be exposed in it, and therefore I believe that Madrid and Spain will have a new emblem, a new tourist reference to be attractive to foreign citizens. On the other hand, alongside the Ministry's activity, I would also like to highlight the programme for support to the Head of State, which will have a budget of €7.7 million, with an increase of 7%. It is basically explained by the reorganization being carried out as a result of the implementation of Royal Decree 297/2022, which we adopted in April this year. It involves the modernization of the structure of the Royal House and also the modernization of its functioning. The objective, as you know, is to strengthen the transparency, accountability, efficiency and exemplaryness of the Royal House, and to that end budgetary allocations are also allocated. Finally, the description of the budget section for which I am responsible ended by referring to the autonomous bodies that are attached to the ministry that I have the honour to lead: firstly, the Centre for Political and Constitutional Studies, with a budget of 4.5 million euros, a minimum increase in the budget that they already have this year; secondly, the Centre for Sociological Research, which will have a budget of 12.66 million euros, an additional 14%, which obviously responds to the electoral processes that we face in 2023, and, thirdly, the budget of the BOE, which maintains its current budget and imports 32.12 million euros. I'm done. We are faced with budgets that give stability in our country. These are the third general budgets of the State that this Government is going to bring forward, with a very large majority of this House, so I take this opportunity to thank all the groups that finally support the general budgets of the State. Thanks to all of them and all of them. [[Applauds]] Perhaps it is time to remember how many other governments had to extend the general budgets of the State when they had the responsibility to govern our country and how many autonomous governments, basically led by conservative parties, have also chosen to extend the accounts during this year. We do not, we are ambitious, we want to transform the country and we believe that the fundamental tool is the approval of the general budgets of the annual state. In addition, it is shown that we are a government whose identity is agreement, dialogue, working with all, seeking points of balance and managing a Parliament like this, where there are different sensibilities but in which the majority of you agree on a common objective, which is to improve the lives of citizens thanks to a very positive general state budget for Spain. These accounts are, therefore, not just a few more budgets, but they are part of a country project, the country project that defends this country, and they are the fundamental tool for building; a country that says yes to continue to advance in front of those who always say no to everything; a country where coexistence is more friendly, where there are agreements, there are bridges of understanding and, therefore, leaves aside those confrontations that polarize society and lead nowhere; a European country, green, social, just, that bets on transition and digitalization, and also a country where politics is made within the Constitution and the law, unlike in other darker stages of our country. That is why, ladies and gentlemen, I ask you to support these general budgets of the State, because they will be improving the lives of citizens. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u49	Thank you very much, President. Thank you very much, ladies and gentlemen. The consensus that democratic memory should arouse in this country... [[Rumors]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u51	... I think it is also fundamental, capital, when we talk about how to invest in modernization and in generating opportunities in the present and in the future. So I go up to this rostrum to present and defend the Draft Law on General Budgets of the State for 2023 regarding section 23, Ministry for the Ecological Transition and the Demographic Challenge, its autonomous agencies and State water societies. I want to do this from the point of view of what we have agreed in the Commission's handling of the amendments that have already been voted on, and I want to do so by stressing that we are continuing to make progress in the consolidation of increasingly greener budgets. These are not only because they constitute a determined policy of this Government, but also because, from the very beginning, we have wanted to highlight it, underlining the priorities established in the field of environmental policies for the transformation of our energy system, the fight against depopulation and the consolidation of equality and the narrowing of the great gap that our country has been experiencing for decades in terms of social and territorial cohesion. We do so with the government's commitment, but we do so also with the conviction that it is the response consistent with the construction of the project and European values. I would say that it has taken us a long time to achieve this presence and this monitoring capacity of the green component in all income and expenditure policies, consolidating the cross-cutting integration of an ecological transition that must be fair and paying attention to those who, at the time of the transformation, may be affected by the profound changes we are experiencing. More than 30% of the total is earmarked for this purpose, and I am referring to the budget of all the ministerial departments once a green budgeting exercise has been carried out for the first time in line with the commitments that you supported when passing the climate change law. We therefore undertake a first-class challenge for Spain and Spain with the world. We tackle the climate crisis, we preserve biodiversity - the most important in Europe - and we innovate in decarbonisation and the fight against pollution; an action backed by 11% more of the budget in 2023 than in 2022, considering both the national budget and the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. With these budgets we realize the response to environmental challenges and their social implications, for which I thank all the groups that accompany us in the Climate Alliance for a prosperity within the environmental limits; an alliance that seeks to transform our productive system and our consumption patterns and promote values that are increasingly compatible with inclusive prosperity, as I said before, for the benefit of people. In fact, we are defending health protection. It is impossible to separate the quality and access to drinking water, waste management, air and soil quality or the good state of biodiversity from human health issues. It is important to emphasize that we always seek to make it compatible with the preservation of nature and ecosystems, which are key to economic development. On a dead planet there is no economic activity, no employment, no impulse to any productive sector. We seek to complement our policies with early adaptation to the clear and modulable effects of climate change, particularly in the approach to water, coastal and sea protection, environment and energy policies. We also want to strengthen our monitoring and observation capacity, not only of the data provided by nature and the climate system, but also to facilitate knowledge and assessment of the impact of the measures being promoted, because it is clear that the extreme weather phenomena we experience more and more often force us to work on scenarios outside the traditional historical average in our country. Similarly, the degree of leadership of new technologies applied to better management, better protection and better preservation of the environment in all their approaches is increasing. An example of this is the adoption of a PERTE for the digitisation of the water cycle. Moreover, in view of the increase in geopolitical and market tensions, special credits are allocated and specific policies are articulated to provide more security against energy prices, which affect in an unorthodox manner the most vulnerable households and the entire productive fabric with measures that contribute to a structural strengthening of energy security and people's security. That is why we are increasing protection for vulnerable consumers, be they households and businesses; we are strengthening strategic and energy autonomy with credits that accelerate the structural changes in our system already under way in the context of the strategic framework for energy and climate, and we are maximizing and creating new infrastructures that will allow for a meaningful exercise of solidarity with the whole of Europe. In short, these are budgets that respond to the challenges of the Ukrainian war scene, which has raised the urgent need to accelerate the transformation of our system, ensuring a much fairer social vision. The budget for the Ministry, without the autonomous agencies and other affiliated entities, amounts to EUR 11,313 million, 11% more than in 2022, of which 6316 correspond to resources from the national budget and 4998 correspond to resources from the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. The allocation of the recovery plan follows the planned three-year schedule and increases from 2022 by 14.1 per cent. It is a budget that allows us to continue to face the great challenges of the country we face. The first is that of the energy transition, a transformation in favour of clean energy at stable, predictable and affordable prices; a scenario in which we have been working since 2018 but which over the past year has achieved a special protagonist. Our well-being, that of families, the competitiveness and the continuity of economic activity explain the commitment for your social support and the impulse to the transformation of the energy system in 2022, as they also do in 2023. The Energy State Secretariat has €4590 million, 70 million more than in 2022, in the share of the regular budget not financed by the recovery plan. Apart from the contribution we must make to the National Commission on Markets and Competition, the budget is increasing by 30.4% to EUR 1076 million. With regard to the recovery plan, the budget amounts to 2950 million, allowing to complete the 20212023 planning that we had marked and that already generates positive impacts on the territory. This budget allows us to deploy the policies that we have been defining to accelerate the energy transition as a central and key element to reduce external energy dependence, reduce negative impacts on our trade balance and replace harmful fossil fuels for our imported health - and therefore in a scenario of high prices, which impoverish us every day - with renewable, indigenous and reliable resources, simultaneously reinforcing consumer protection measures - especially, as I pointed out, the most vulnerable - and strongly supporting our industrial fabric. As in 2022, we have sufficient flexibility to incorporate the Government's support for measures to reduce energy costs, thus facilitating their sustainability for families, businesses and industries. I would like to highlight, among other rules, the recent effort made with the last royal decree law, which involves providing 3000 million euros to the TUR 4 tariff, the neighborhood; 187 million for the flexibility of industrial gas contracts, and 225 million euros additional for the thermal social bonus. Therefore, I would like to highlight the effort to cover much of the year 2023, helping in this context of rising prices to facilitate the thermal comfort of our homes. We continue to believe that we are part of the global vanguard of future technologies - renewable energy, green hydrogen and digitalisation of our networks -, also seeking to attract employment, strategic autonomy and innovation in our industrial sectors and value chains. The budget includes major transfers amounting to more than EUR 4 billion, of which EUR 3514 correspond to transfers to the National Commission on Markets and Competition for the energy system, EUR 2862 to finance electricity market costs from CO2 and other green taxes and EUR 646 million for energy extra-cost in island territories. In addition, $6.3 million is budgeted as a transfer to the Commission to pay for underground storage. The key element of the energy transition is, however, the implementation of the development of renewable energies, ensuring their social and environmental sustainability in the territory. In these budgets we do not forget to mitigate the impact for the most vulnerable. We expect EUR 259 million for the thermal social bond, which represents an increase of 102 million more than in the initial budget of 2022, i.e. almost 65% more than last year. We pay particular attention to leading a fair transition and, therefore, the budget managed by the Institute for the Just Transition will grow this year to EUR 501 million and 169 million in transfers from the recovery plan, of which 66 million will be earmarked for employment-generating projects, 139 million for the closure plan for private and public enterprises and 95 million for agreements with autonomous communities to encourage alternative activity in these regions. [[Rumors.-Pause]] Thank you. They are credits with an obvious social dimension. They promote alternative economic projects and developments in areas very affected by changes and transformation towards a new energy model, because they had traditionally been linked to coal and deserve an alternative, a different expectation. The 169 million of the recovery plan for just transition will be used for environmental restoration, development of an infrastructure plan, implementation of energy R & D+i projects and implementation of a support plan for job qualification and integration. In addition, the Fair Transition Fund will be funded with 41 million from the European Union fund for this purpose. Finally, in relation to the recovery plan, in 2023 we want to continue the programmes with the greatest demand so far and we have activated the last instruments we were designing in these months. This includes €780 million for the innovative renewable energy development plan integrated into building and production processes. This will allow us to continue to reduce our energy dependence, giving alternatives to businesses and households to replace conventional electricity needs, such as air conditioning or even process heat, with renewable solutions. The same component also includes another €25 million for sustainable energy on islands, continuing important work carried out jointly with the autonomous governments of the Balearic and Canary Islands. To ensure the proper integration of renewables we must continue to deepen the modernization of our energy system, so the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan allocates 94 million for the storage of electricity, flexibility and the impulse of new business models. The plan includes an additional €673 million for renewable hydrogen, which will allow us to complete the framework for support for unique projects, valleys or hydrogen clusters, and to respond to the framework for EU-supported HIPCs. 20% of new renewable hydrogen projects are in our territory. In addition to the work carried out by the Ministry of Transport, Mobility and Urban Agenda on the change of mobility and the promotion of sustainable cities, we allocated EUR 455 million in 2023 to the installation of charging points, the acquisition of electric vehicles and fuel cells and the innovation of electromobility, charging and green hydrogen, as well as to the promotion of energy efficiency in the General Administration of the State and in smaller municipalities. The rehabilitation plan in the area of the demographic challenge has a budget of 225 million euros for the rehabilitation programme in PRE 5000 buildings and for sustainable urban development in municipalities with less than 5000 inhabitants. In addition, there are 504 million for the Energy Transition Plan in the State Administration, 23 of which are reflected in the Mixteco budget and 481 in those of other departments and agencies of the State Administration. As a novelty, I would like to point out that EUR 4.6 million is allocated for actions in the road map for the sustainable management of mineral raw materials, perhaps one of the areas in which we must drive something that has been standing for decades: to understand and sustainably manage our mineral raw materials. Knowing where they are and under what conditions they can be extracted in their case are prerequisites for designing a national policy of raw materials that guarantees strategic autonomy, but that is simultaneously respectful of environmental and social constraints. This road map supports investments under the recovery plan. These include the electric vehicle PART, ecosystem restoration and green infrastructure, the restoration of degraded mining sites, the 2030 industrial drive strategy, and chip and battery strategies for research. The second major challenge we face is the ecological one, which generates human activity in the natural environment by introducing pollution elements, inducing greater climate change or the loss of biodiversity in our ecosystems. The Secretariat of State for the Environment and its Directorates-General and Agencies have a budget of €4460 million in 2023, €909 million more than in 2022, which is 25.6% above last year's budget. Of these, 1957 million come from the recovery plan and 2503 from the regular financing of the general budgets of the State. 247 million are earmarked for the autonomous bodies attached: hydrographic confederations, Commonwealth of the Taibilla Canals, State Meteorology Agency and the autonomous body of National Parks, together with 44 million allocated and managed by the Biodiversity Foundation. This latter foundation received 33 million more than in 2022, of which 25 will be used to finance actions from European funds - 22 million ERDF and 3 million from the recovery mechanism - and 8 additional to take over the management of important resources from both funds. The Directorate-General for Biodiversity, Forests and Desertification has a budget of 216 million, to which will have to be added 313.6 of the recovery plan, so in total it will have to manage 535.8 million euros. They highlight the lines of action aimed at digitising and improving the knowledge of the natural environment, with 72 million euros; the boost to the protection of marine and terrestrial biodiversity, with 155 million euros; as well as the aid to farmers and preventive actions for the conservation and preservation of large carnivores, which are maintained at 20 million euros annually. In addition, another EUR 2 million is added for the conservation of the Iberian lynx. For the restoration of ecosystems we have 94 million, also coming from the recovery plan, and highlight some projects as important as the actions envisaged in the framework of priority actions for the Sea Minor, the protection of Doñana or the Ebro Delta. Sustainable forest management has 144 million euros from the regular budget and 97 from the recovery plan. The national budget is increasing by EUR 14 million, as a reflection of the growing concern that forces us to strengthen measures against forest fires throughout the year. Resources are also allocated for the updating and modernization of at least 10 fire-extinguishing aircraft and for the operation of the biodiversity knowledge monitoring and management system, as well as for the modernization of the bases of the Forest Fire Enforcement Brigades that complement the competence of the autonomous communities. These budgets seek to implement the European Union's biodiversity strategy, the updating and implementation of the Strategic Plan for Natural Heritage and Biodiversity, the forestry strategy and plan, support for autonomous communities in the conservation and recovery of threatened species or the conservation and management of marine biodiversity. For the Autonomous National Parks Agency 60.3 million euros are budgeted, 13 more than in 2022, for investments among which are those of consolidation of protected spaces, with 11 million euros financed by the European Regional Development Fund. And to these figures are added 101.5 million euros of the recovery mechanism, which represents a total budget for this autonomous body of 161.8 million euros, increasing by 94 % compared to 2022. Undoubtedly, the item with a greater increase in the ministry's budget. The Water Directorate-General will have €2082 million in budget and an additional 1055 from the recovery plan. The main lines of investment focus on sanitation and purification, restoration and conservation of the water public domain, defence against flooding, maintenance of support for hydrographic confederations and strengthening and development of innovation in water management. Sanitation and purification have been allocated EUR 412 million. I want to highlight such important projects in the territory as the sanitation and purification of the Alto Nervión, the continuation of the expansion of the Buenos Aires wastewater treatment plant in Tenerife; the sewage treatment plants of Albacete, Ibiza or Soria or the Plasencia sanitation system. And to this figure we have to add an additional 51 million euros that manage the hydrographic confederations under this heading. For the restoration and conservation of the hydraulic public domain and defense against floods we allocate a total of 763 million between the regular budget and funds of the recovery plan. To this we add 995 million euros that manage the hydrographic confederations. In particular, EUR 94.78 million is earmarked for the security, maintenance and maintenance of infrastructure, a heading which was dramatically undermined in the years of the previous crisis and which obliges us to strengthen the security of all our facilities, in particular those of dams. For the digital transition in the water sector, there is an investment of 370 million from the recovery mechanism, which joins 105 of the current budget of 2022. The aim is to increase knowledge, improve the use of resources and ensure the most efficient management of water, avoiding leakage and ensuring the necessary use with all guarantees by administrations and users. To these resources are added 284 million euros of investment for the state companies Aquaes and Aquamed, previewed for 2023. Let us think that all these water programmes are closely linked to what will be the third water planning cycle, which will have more resources than our country has ever been able to manage. As for the Normative Programme and Territorial Planning of Water Resources, with an amount of EUR 386.82 million in horizontal activities for the acquisition of public sector shares and participations, we know that this represents an increase of 286 million, destined almost entirely to the State society Aquamed, allowing competitive prices in the production of desalinated water at the present time of high energy prices and thus favoring the stability of the price of water and the possibility that desalinated water can be acquired by users at competitive prices. The budget of the Directorate-General of the Coast and Sea will have 72 million regular budget and 140 of the recovery plan, in order to add a total of 212 million euros that allow us to allocate 5,5 to ensure the integrity of the terrestrial maritime public domain; 41.7 for the protection and conservation of the coast; 16.8 to ensure the public use and enjoyment of this space and 7.8 to plan, conserve and improve the marine environment. Adaptation to climate change and increased resilience are essential factors for planning actions against erosion and coastal strengthening, so we will invest EUR 3 million in creating green filters to prevent pollution from the Sea Minor and EUR 1.9 million for aid to shellfish farmers in the Burgo region, thus compensating for the inactivity caused by actions financed by the ERDF that have been being implemented in this area for some months now. The actions of this programme are based on effective collaboration between all administrations, in many cases directly focusing on competencies that are the responsibility of other levels of administration. Depending on this flexibility, the urgency and the different strategies that are being promoted from the territory, we will apply the budget lines in the different areas of the coast. The Directorate-General for Quality and Environmental Assessment has a total budget of 266 million euros to guarantee, among other actions, the circular economy strategy. 200 million are for lines of subsidies that drive the circular economy in the field of enterprise and transfers to autonomous communities amounting to EUR 49 million under the recovery mechanism. In addition, there is a regular budget allocation of €7.5 million for transfers to autonomous communities for the implementation of waste regulations and the promotion of separation and drive to the circular economy. The Spanish Climate Change Office will have 25 million euros in budget. It highlights the contribution of 12.8 million to the Carbon Fund, which funds projects to reduce CO2 emissions, and the transfer of €7.5 million to autonomous communities for the implementation of actions under the PIMA Plan, climate shelters. I want to devote the last part of this journey through the budgets of the ministry to the third, but no less important, challenge of the drive to policies to meet the demographic challenge and depopulation. A budget that is aimed at promoting and developing in a complementary way the competences of the autonomous communities and sectoral interventions from other ministries in policies to combat territorial vulnerability and the boost of the territorial and social cohesion of Spain. Innovation and entrepreneurship, with special emphasis on young people and women; new technologies and facilitating access to services, housing and connectivity are some of the points of interest. Social development, equal access to public services, the generation of rural economies, the gender perspective and the presence of the State in the territory appear as clear approaches in this budget. The budget of the Directorate-General for Policies against Depopulation amounts to EUR 71 million. Among the main programmes of this Directorate-General, the Cohesion and Territorial Transformation Fund with 28.4 million euros, orienting its investments to the financing of tractor projects that allow the impulse and sustainability of municipalities with greater vulnerability, smaller size and more innovative vocation. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u91	Thank you very much, President. Good morning, ladies and gentlemen. It is an honour for me to appear before the Plenary of the Congress of Deputies to explain the broad lines of the Draft Law on General Budgets of the State of 2023 in the part that corresponds to section 20 of the Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism. Before I enter the large figures in the budget, I would first like to refer to the current situation. We are, as you know, at a complex time. Putin's war has created an added uncertainty to which we already had. The difficult geopolitical situation is affecting the world economy and also the Spanish economy, and that is why our priority since the beginning of the war has been to help our productive fabric, to protect it above all against rising energy prices. Everything we are doing is also building on the strength of dialogue and agreement. Since this inflationary cycle began, the Government has been responding decisively and ambitiously, adopting successive fiscal and regulatory packages that are enabling us to cushion the impact of inflation and thereby maintain solid growth. We are seeing how effective the measures of the Government are. We can see this in the containment of inflation, which we are seeing in the last three months and also in the intense job creation and in the reduction of unemployment. I would like to share with you three data that support these statements. Spain recorded the second lowest annual inflation rate in the entire euro area in October, Spain leads the growth of the euro area, with a forecast for this year of 4.4%, and Spain is one of the major European economies, which has had the greatest increase in employment since the pandemic. Social Security membership, as you know, is at historical levels, around 20.3 million occupied, the highest figure since 2008. Therefore, from prudence in the face of such uncertainty, we can say that the Spanish economy is reasonably resisting a context, as I said, as complex as the current one. The 2023 budgets are therefore an economic policy instrument necessary to address the current context of uncertainty, but also to boost the necessary investments and reforms that contribute to economic and social growth and the generation of quality jobs. I shall now detail to you the budget for 2023 of the Ministry of Industry, Commerce and Tourism, a budget amounting to EUR 10 031 million, the largest budget, ladies and gentlemen, in the history of industry, trade and tourism, an increase of 21.4% over the budgets of 2022. With this budget, the ministry continues the path of productive investment that has an effect on our economic growth and employment. Continuity is therefore given to the transformation and modernization of industry, trade and tourism, with the impetus of the change in the productive model. As you know, as in previous years, the budget incorporates the investments of components 12 and 13 of the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. In total, this corresponds to EUR 6258 million of European funds. In the area of industry and SMEs we are trying to consolidate with this budget a model of balanced, solid, inclusive and sustainable economic growth; Spain is clearly aligned with the guidelines of the European agenda to make an ambitious industrial policy and position itself among the first European economies in the two major transformations, such as the ecological and digital transition, so that the objectives of industrialization and modernization of our country are met. Spain is demonstrating its attractiveness as an investor destination thanks to major strategic projects such as the electric vehicle, renewable energy or semiconductor projects. The proposed industrial and SME budgets have a total budget of 7243 million, an increase of 26.1% over this year's budget. Ladies and gentlemen, to give you an idea, when I arrived at the ministry, the budget for 2018 was 2152 million euros. Therefore, next year we will have three times as many resources as we find ourselves, I insist, when we arrive at the Government. This ambitious project will enable us to strengthen our industrial and reindustrialization policy in our country and will undoubtedly be a key element in improving our productive model, adapting it to technological and climate change, also contributing to sustainable and inclusive economic growth. As you know, the recovery plan is structured, in the area of industry and SMEs, around the actions provided for in components 12 and 13. Component 12, Spain's industrial policy 2030, places the emphasis on the modernization and sustainability of industry and the drive for tractor projects, and will have a budget of €43 billion. Component 13, SME drive, through which each and every measure aimed at improving the competitiveness of our small and medium-sized enterprises is articulated, will have a budget of EUR 308 million. The following lines of action will also be developed in 2023: the line of support for strategic projects for economic recovery and transformation, the PERTE, which is the one with the greatest scope within the programme. In 2021, as you know, the share of the electric and connected vehicle and of the state-of-the-art health has been approved, and in 2022 the agri-food, circular economy, for the shipbuilding industry, microelectronics and semiconductors have been approved, and it is also expected that before the end of the year we will approve the share of the decarbonisation of the industry. The first two have already called for aid that is the responsibility of the ministry, and we anticipate that in the coming weeks we will also approve the call for the naval and agri-food loss. The electric vehicle PART, as you know, has mobilised aid in its first call for EUR 877 million, becoming the largest economic industrial programme in the history of support for our automotive industry. A new call is planned for 2023, which will undoubtedly help to consolidate our electromobility ecosystem at European level. The total allocation of the 2023 General State Budgets for all PERTE is EUR 3808 million; 62% of this amount is earmarked for grants, and thus we respond to the demand that is being made from industry. In addition to PERTE, we continue to develop other lines of action, focusing on the support to the ecological and energy transition of our industry and also to its digital transformation, through the line of innovation projects and organisational and R & D processes, with a allocation for the next year of 128 million euros and also the line of implementation of the digitization of organizational processes in industrial companies, which is endowed for next year with 90 million euros. Apart from the actions under the recovery plan, I would also like to highlight the increase in the CO2 indirect cost compensation programme for the coming year with EUR 244 million, which represents a 36% increase over the allocation we have this year. They are aware that, in addition, in the regulations of the General Budgets of the State it is envisaged that we can increase this item with revenues from greenhouse gas emission allowances auctions as provided for in the Climate Change Act. The allocation of fee compensation, provided for under the Statute of the E-Intensive Consumer, is also maintained, with an allocation for the coming year of EUR 91 million. Within the budget, I would also like to highlight other programmes in support of industrial investment, such as the Industrial Productive Investment Support Fund, which is intended to finance development projects and industrial enterprise growth projects, which for the third year in a row will have an additional EUR 600 million. So far, ladies and gentlemen, this fund has mobilised EUR 483 million. We have financed thirty-three projects with a total investment of 1 billion and the creation of 2043 direct jobs. The second is the support that we provide from the Ministry of Industry to the defence and aerospace sector, a sector that has a strategic character within the industrial activities for its great tractor effect on other activities, for the high components of export, innovation, productivity and added value that are made through the repayable contributions to companies for the implementation of technological and industrial projects with the defense programs and that next year it has an allocation of 1601 million euros. I now turn to the main developments of the budget in the area of trade for the year 2023. In general terms, the budget amounts to EUR 1640 million, which represents an increase of more than EUR 964 million over last year and an increase in annual terms of 142.7 %, thus consolidating with the growth we had been having in previous years. Let me detail some of the actions we will be taking with the Trade budget next year. I would like to highlight the non-reimbursable funding line for feasibility studies under the EIF, with an additional 20 million; the actions being carried out by ICEX, which amount to EUR 52.9 million; the international mentoring project, which is being launched by the Chamber of Commerce and whose objective is to support internationalised Spanish SMEs, increase their potential by increasing exports and consolidate and diversify markets with large Spanish companies and multinationals, but also supporting small and medium-sized enterprises. In the year 2023 we will devote an additional 13 million to this programme, in addition to the 32 million that we are developing this year. In addition to these actions, which are already under way, in 2023 we will put into operation a new instrument, the Co-Investment Fund with Sovereign Institutions, which will have a budget of 950 million euros from the Recovery and Resilience Mechanism. This fund will be managed by COFIDES and aims to promote the attraction of foreign investment through the co-investment of the Spanish State with sovereign institutions and foreign institutional investments. In the area of internal trade, the recovery mechanism will amount to more than EUR 156 million. All actions in this area are already under way. In the year 2023 will be published new calls of the lines of aid from municipalities to small businesses, which have worked very well throughout the year 2021-2022, along with new transfers to autonomous communities to channel the resources of the technological fund, which for next year will have 50 million euros. As for the area of Tourism, they collect another year of investment from European funds and allow us to continue to drive the recovery of our tourism sector. As you know, the trend of recovery of the tourism sector remains in the autumn and the most robust indicator - the thermometer that allows us to measure this intense recovery - is employment. The number of Social Security affiliates linked to tourism activities in October has marked the best in its historical series, exceeding 2.5 million workers. The tourism budgets will mobilise EUR 1092 million, which also means a significant development of the planning that we have included in component 14 of the recovery plan, the plan for modernisation and competitiveness of the tourism sector. This is an unprecedented volume of resources for tourism policy for the third consecutive year, which responds to a clear and recognizable model, based on a change of model towards sustainability, modernization, the competitiveness of our destinations and the tourism ecosystem operating in them. Our tourism policy is structured around four axes: sustainability, digitisation, resilience and competitiveness, and it is on these four axes where the budget rests. There is full coherence between these fundamentals the Tourism Sector Modernisation and Competitiveness Plan and our budgets. We will develop a new extraordinary call for tourism sustainability plans in destination, in the year 2023 it will have a budget of 478 million euros, of which around 33 million will be reserved to a national priority, which will add to the one that we have already developed in the year 2021, the Xacobeo 2021-2022; in the year 2022, the National Plan of Enogastronomic Tourism, and in 2023, it will be agreed in the framework of the sectoral conference that we will hold before the end of the year. We can highlight the good reception that this program has had, the tourism sustainability plans received in the year 2012504 requests, of which we have financed 153, with an average budget by plan of 3.2 million euros. In the current call that we are resolving right now we have received 514 requests and we hope, indeed, to reconfigure and articulate our tourism policy around these major tractor plans. We have also reinforced the regular call for tourism sustainability plans, bringing back 23 million euros. Budgets also include social sustainability plans, with EUR 6.8 million, which will also enable us to improve the comprehensive tourism sustainability system with more than EUR 10 million. These plans, which we are working in coordination with the social partners, aim to highlight and understand the good practices in the field of work, corporate social responsibility, which many of our players in the tourism sector are already developing. The second axis of action is digitisation and tourist intelligence, with an investment for the next year that exceeds 96 million euros and that is structured around two major items: digitisation of destinations, 41.92 million, and digital skills, more than 53 million. We will continue during the year 2023 developing the Tourist Intelligence System to convert it into a data aggregator that modifies the tourist transformation of our destinations at the national level, allowing to generate control frameworks of tourism in real time, both for destinations and for the tourist ecosystem, in order to be able to have better information in their decision-making. We are also launching the Intelligent Destination Platform, which, as you know, has its headquarters in Benidorm; a unique pioneering program, which aims to connect the tourist with the offer to the destination in an integral way, with all the actors who interact in a space that is already digital and that can be measured and scaled. The third axis of our tourism policy is resilience focused on extrapenisular destinations. Next year we will count on a contribution of 30 million euros in the autonomous communities to continue developing, together with the 70 million that we have already made available to these communities in the years 2021-2022, a strategy of modernization of their sector from different perspectives. We will also finance the autonomous cities of Ceuta and Melilla with an additional 2 million, which will certainly allow us to strengthen the resilience of these extrapenisular territories, the Canary Islands, the Balearic Islands, Ceuta and Melilla, which were most affected by the pandemic because of their vulnerability. The fourth axis is the competitiveness of our companies, of our destinations. We will continue to develop successful programmes, such as the Tourism Experiences Programme, which will have a budget of 22.5 million euros for the coming year and which will once again allow us to deploy a set of actions throughout the whole territory to develop and enhance our tourism products. A few days ago we published the final resolution of 2021. We have benefited 224 public and private entities, with a total of 36 selected experiences from more than 900 applications we received. Once again we see the acceptance of the programmes that we are launching from the Secretariat of State for Tourism. We also renew our commitment to energy efficiency, the circularity of the sector, through new aid to the productive sector of our tourism; in 2023 we will launch a new line with 43 million euros. It ended with the last axis, modernization, the advancement of the artistic and cultural heritage of our country. Culture and heritage, without doubt, is one of the greatest attractions of tourists to visit us. We have already invested some €400 million in rehabilitation, improvement, conservation of tourist properties, and in 2023 we will launch a new line to strengthen our cultural and artistic heritage throughout the national territory, with a budget of €89 million. I'm finishing up, ladies and gentlemen. These budgets that I come to present to you today deepen the path of recovery and modernization of our productive model, to face the challenges of the green and digital double transition, to contribute to the generation of employment, but also to contribute to social and territorial cohesion and to the narrowing of the gender gap. If this draft budget comes forward and receives the majority support of this House, we will launch an image of stability that will undoubtedly lead to greater strength and credibility in Spain. I therefore thank the parliamentary groups that have expressed their support for these budgets and appeal to the rest of the groups to support with their vote the budgets that will allow us to transform and modernise our country. I would like once again to reiterate my commitment to reaching the necessary agreements that contribute to this objective, which is shared. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u117	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, as it is mandatory, I rise to the rostrum to defend the Draft Law on General Budgets of the State for the year 2023 in relation to section 24, Ministry of Culture and Sport, and let me tell you that I do so with the firm conviction that they are very good budgets. As I have already said in the presentation of the budget for this year, we in the Government have conceived the budget as a key tool for strengthening culture and sport in our country because it is an important lever to achieve this goal. Culture is an essential part of our personal development and the development of our society and can and must play a crucial role in building a better society. Culture is an industry that generates wealth and employment with enormous growth potential both nationally and internationally, produces economic and social benefits and is a source of emotional well-being and health. As the Minister of Finance and Public Service, María Jesús Montero, pointed out in her appearance before the plenary of this House on October 26 to defend the draft budgets, culture is one of the sectors that has suffered most during this pandemic and has a primary role in building societies that want to know how advanced, but also critical and free. I fully agree with the Minister's assessment and believe that the budgets for culture and sport are helping to promote the growth of this industrial sector, its productivity and the competitiveness of these sectors, generating jobs and also helping to solve or alleviate the precarious economic situation in which professionals and companies in these sectors sometimes move. Ladies and gentlemen, I want to start my speech - I already know that the debate is sometimes based on realities and strictly on opinions - highlighting what I think is most relevant, which is the overall figure, the EUR 1804 million that the Spanish Government, if the House approves the budgets, will allocate to cultural and sports policies. It is the largest budget in the entire historical series, with a 13.5 % increase over this year's figures. This figure actually corresponds to the whole of the cultural and sports policy and therefore includes the part directly under the Ministry of Culture and Sport, but also incorporates the shares corresponding to the Cervantes Institute, National Heritage or Spanish Cultural Action. The budgets therefore consolidate a continuous increase in culture and sport since Pedro Sánchez accepted the Presidency of the Government and also - and I think this is a very important thing - for the first time give culture and sport the consideration of public goods of an essential nature, precisely because of their vertebrate nature of society. As I said, of the total of 1804 million euros, 1703 will be managed directly by the Ministry of Culture and Sport and of these, 296 million euros correspond to the Mechanism of Recovery, Transformation and Resilience. In effect, we are faced with an ambitious budget proposal, which aims to contribute to social reconstruction, to vertebration, to social and territorial cohesion and to the consolidation of the cultural and sporting renaissance that is already taking place and that must, if I may express it, strengthen and strengthen itself in 2023. These budgets pursue four main objectives: supporting the industrial fabric and contributing to its strengthening; facilitating access to culture for all citizens; social and economic dynamism in rural areas, promoting processes of rooting and fixing the population, and promoting new cultural and sporting habits, as well as creating new audiences, especially among the youngest, which is the strongest guarantee of the future and progress of societies. It is about taking advantage of everything we have learned during these years of crisis, first with the pandemic and then with the war - because, yes, ladies and gentlemen, there was a pandemic and there is a war - to advance being stronger, more creative, more prepared and with more culture and sport. Thus, the budgets once again provide for a 210 million budget to finance the young cultural bonus which, as you know, wants to support that embrace between young people and culture and culture and young people, and I must say to them - I think I said it already in the House Committee on Culture - that 281,557 young people have requested it and obtained it this year. Those born in 2005, approximately half a million, will be able to apply next year. This time we will still be able to improve the knowledge mechanisms of this programme and the management mechanisms. The aim is also to promote cultural activity throughout the territory, to facilitate the access of culture to all citizens, to stimulate our rural environment socially and economically and to promote processes of rooting and fixing the population. This will also strengthen the cultural sectors that also have that economic component of which I spoke and will therefore strengthen the employment, fabric and cultural ecosystem, both public and private, that operates throughout the territory. We will take advantage of this year's experience to improve the programme with the aim of making it even more young people and more businesses and cultural entities that can benefit from a programme that also benefits society as a whole. Our aim is that, without prejudice to the guarantees necessary for the proper use of public money, both by the young beneficiaries and by the affiliated companies, it will be possible to speed up the processing of the money and to exceed the figures of this first call. We are convinced that a cult country and a country that embraces and lets itself be embraced by culture is a better country. Ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to highlight the important commitment that these budgets make to strengthen the staff at the disposal of the Ministry of Culture and Sport in order to be able to manage better and to offer the best service to citizens. Now I will tell you the figures, but I think that sometimes things are explained much better graphically. This is the evolution of public employment offers in terms of culture and sport, which have experienced in recent years a very significant growth, logically accompanied by the growth that has also had the budgets of the department that I honor to direct. [[shows some graphs]] [[Applauds]] More culture, more sport, more budget and more personal. In particular, the project I am presenting envisages an increase of EUR 10 million over this year. This will result in the incorporation of more than six hundred workers, more than six hundred public employees, with the aim of further strengthening the structure of cultural institutions. I would also like to point out, because it was the subject of intense debates on the occasion of the debate in the Chamber of Audiovisual Law, that for the first time the Fund for the Protection of Cinematography exceeds the figure of 100 million euros, which was a demand very felt by the sector. In fact, we moved from EUR 70 million in the 2022 budget to EUR 107 million in the 2023 budget. This will boost the financing of competitive production aid calls. But this does not exhaust the contribution of public funds to the audiovisual sector, we also allocate another 39 million euros for the autonomous communities to manage support to the audiovisual sector in three lines. First, promotion of cinematography in official languages, 10 million euros, a fund that recovered last year because it had been lost in previous stages, I don't know why, and that is maintained in 2023. Secondly, aid to cinemas, EUR 14.5 million, because here, ladies and gentlemen, I think it will agree with me that the cultural sectors are gradually recovering from the pandemic, but it is precisely the cinemas that have suffered the most from the drop in spectators and have not yet recovered the number of spectators they had before the pandemic. The fundamental objective is to modernize our facilities and revolutionize Olympic and Paralympic preparations. The resources from the recovery funds represent 30 per cent of the year 2022 and are in line with the criteria of the funds. Therefore, 55.2 million will be targeted at digitisation; 69 million towards the ecological transition and 16 million towards the Social Plan for the Sport Sector, which develops plans for the training and visibility of women's sport, which is experiencing a very significant boom and which must count on our full support because it is destined to go even further. The government promised last year that in the four years of the legislature it would reach a billion euros of investment in sport. Some of your ladies and gentlemen, and I can understand it, have been skeptical, but I must tell you that with the figures from the 2023 budget we have surpassed that milestone and reached EUR 1149 million in four years. Therefore, we are faced with budgets that want to continue to promote sport as a factor of social cohesion and that continue to think of sport as a lever of equality, and hence the importance for us of women's sport; and also not only that, but that will depend on your lordships with the final approval of the law of sport, full equality in the sports and federal structures of our sport. I should like to thank you for the amendments that have been incorporated. I always have that conviction, which has been fulfilled in the last two years, that a good draft budget is in place and is improved and, in this case, it is also in place. I would therefore like to thank your ladies and gentlemen for the efforts they have made and because there has clearly been in some cases a prioritisation of culture and sport which we are all going to benefit from. I am going to ask all of you to vote in favour, although I already know that this is a request that is probably not going to be successful. It is true that it is always possible to do things better, it is always possible to go further, it is always possible to improve some aspects that are often recurrent in interventions, and I must tell you that we are greatly improving the degree of budget implementation. In any case, I believe that budgets are a clear signal to all the institutions and to Spanish society. We believe in culture, we believe in sport, we believe in sportsmen and creators, and we are going to mobilize a historical amount of resources so that they can carry out their work in freedom, in full competition and dedicated to the enjoyment of all, because in the end the recipients of culture and sport are not only those who practice them, but the whole society that has the right to enjoy them. That is why it is so important not only to strengthen our libraries, our cinemas, our museums and libraries, but to bring them closer to people. I cannot agree more, and I would recommend the same to our President of the Government and to our Minister, but I would add a few women and, in addition, women who write in the different languages of the State. For, as bad as it is for many, our tongues are also our wealth. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u148	Thank you very much. We continue with the debate in section 18, Ministry of Education and Vocational Training. The Minister for Education and Vocational Training, on behalf of the Government, has the floor for the section's presentation.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u149	Thank you very much, Mr President. Good morning, almost good afternoon, to your ladies and gentlemen. I appear before the plenary of this House to report on the draft budgets of the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training for the coming year 2023, which corresponds, as the President said, to section 18 of the general budgets of the State. Obviously, the budget of this ministry shares the objectives of the country that marks the Draft Law on General State Budgets by 2023. Education can - it is already making - make an important contribution to the recovery of the economy by increasing the average level of training of the whole population, reducing early school leaving, increasing vocational training graduates and updating and recycling the professional skills of workers. Education also contributes to the transformation of our productive system by creating new vocational training qualifications in cutting-edge technologies and by increasing the digital skills of all pupils. But, being important, I believe that the most valuable contribution should be the provision of a quality public education service that gives all children, all girls and young people the maximum opportunity to develop their abilities and talents to the fullest, regardless of their socio-economic situation and personal circumstances. However, today we are here to talk not only about our purposes, but above all about the resources that we are going to devote to making this all a reality. Of course, I am very pleased as Minister of the Government of Spain to come to this House with the third consecutive budget in time and also in form, which will allow us to continue to make progress in improving our education system. This government is completing one of the most reformist and transformative legislatures of our democracy, because today, ladies and gentlemen, we have a better country, with more rights, more social justice, better public services, more growth and more employment. In that connection, this draft budget also culminates in a period of historic investment in education. It is true that all governments always say that education is a key part of building a future in our country, but not always the facts accompany good intentions. That can easily be understood with a single data. In 2018, the Ministry of Education invested in Spain for each student, for each student, 315 euros - in 2018, 315 euros per student - while today, in 2023, this government, the Ministry of Education and Vocational Training, invests in each student, in each student, 634 euros, more than double. In 2018, 315 euros; in 2023, 634 euros. [[Applauds]] The opinion before the plenary for its vote also provides for a budget of EUR 6408 million, which is 6.15% more than the financial year 2022. Of this total, 5122 million go to education policies, 1252 to training for employment and over 11 million to education abroad. As for the sources of funding, the project includes 1264 million European funds, which makes the national financing of our budget amount to 5144 million, with an increase of 17.35 %. In addition, in the initial section of the large numbers of budgets, it is noteworthy that more than a third of them will be channelled through transfers to the autonomous communities, with a figure of 2350 million euros. Although then, logically, I will go further in this respect, I cannot fail to mention here the departure of the most significant ministry for its amount, that of scholarships and study grants, with a total of 2549 million euros; that is, we will devote 4 out of 10 euros to equal opportunities. In short, ladies and gentlemen, this budget consolidates a legislature in which the foundations have been laid for Spain to get closer to the level of educational investment that we have as one of the most important countries in Europe. Leaving behind the explanation of the large numbers, I shall now point out to you the specific objectives and priorities of our budget for 2023. As you are well aware, we have been giving strategic impetus throughout the whole legislature to four lines of action: the reform and modernisation of the education system, the strengthening of policies of equity and the compensation of inequalities, the profound transformation of vocational training and, finally, digitalisation. With regard to the reform and modernization of the education system, it is mainly based on normative work and negotiation with the autonomous communities and other stakeholders. And the other three strategic lines I have mentioned also have a very prominent presence in the budgets of 2023. With regard to the strengthening of policies on equity and the compensation of inequalities, it will absorb 45% of the department's budget. As I have just said, scholarship policy is undoubtedly the flagship of this department, with the significant increase proposed for 2023 of an additional 18.62%, with a total figure of up to EUR 2549 million. This remarkable increase is part of the measures that the Government has been adopting over the course of this year to alleviate the effects of inflation on family economies, which in this case allow the goal of no student having to leave school because of the crisis resulting from the war in Ukraine. Fellowships are one of the main instruments to ensure access to education at non-compulsory stages and are one of the mechanisms that make real equality of opportunity for study possible. The effort made by this Government is indisputable. In 2023, the scholarship budget will be 66% higher than in 2018. [[Applauds]] We are talking about an additional 1 billion in 2023 over what was invested in 2018. In addition to a much higher amount, there has been an increase in the number of beneficiaries, and at the moment we are talking about almost a million young people accessing scholarships. We have also increased the average amount of aid, especially for young people in rural areas with displacement needs, and the application and grant process has also been speeded up, so that from this course all students, before enrolling, know whether they meet the economic requirements for scholarship. Apart from scholarships, there are other aid programmes, among which I would like to highlight that the textbook coverage programme will be increased by EUR 10 million, which will reach EUR 58 million. In addition to the effort to ensure access to education in the mandatory stages, it is equally important to provide compensatory support and reinforcements that facilitate the learning of all our students, overcoming the difficulties they may have for socio-economic reasons or for personal and family circumstances. To make this true inclusive and equitable education possible, the budget also includes a very positive impact on what we know as the PROA programme, which will have a budget of more than EUR 120 million for educational reinforcement actions in centres of special complexity, as well as the programme for the creation of units of personal and family support and guidance, with almost EUR 56 million, with which it is expected to create a thousand new units of guidance. We will also continue with the programme for the creation of places in the first cycle of children's education, which will be endowed with 136 million euros, and we also include important mechanisms for inclusion and equity, such as educational inclusion and emotional well-being programmes, which will have 37 million euros and 5 million euros respectively. The second strategic objective of our action in budgetary terms is to transform and modernise vocational training. Ladies and gentlemen, you are well aware of the broad social, business and educational support this structural reform has gained, which will also serve to provide access to quality employment for thousands of young people and also to enable companies to find those professional profiles that are now lacking. In addition, integration into this unique system of vocational education and employment training has created a much more effective and coherent scheme to enable the continuing training of all workers in our country. That is why we can say that vocational training has become a fundamental strategy for growth and employment. That is why it is also one of the great milestones of this legislature, and this has been reflected in the budgets since 2021. For this 2023 the total resources to be allocated for this purpose for vocational training amount to EUR 2401 million, representing more than 37% of the overall budget of the department. Of this amount, EUR 947 million will be financed through European funds, while EUR 1178 million will be earmarked for training measures for workers and EUR 276 million for vocational training measures for young people. Among the objectives to be achieved with this budget in 2023, in the field of vocational training we can highlight the creation, at least, of 50 500 new vocational training places and the conversion into bilingual of 37,000 others, opening at least seven hundred Mentor classrooms in municipalities with less than five thousand inhabitants, the creation of 180 digital simulators, publishing twenty new titles and specialization courses, opening 450 entrepreneurship classrooms, adapting a minimum of 213 applied technology classrooms, accrediting skills acquired by work experience to at least five hundred thousand people or training 125,000 active digital workers. Finally, the department's third strategic objective in budgetary terms is digitization, which has also become an essential process for all educational systems. To this end, we will allocate more than EUR 550 million in this budget year. This budget also includes as the main innovation the programme School Code 4.0, which will have a budget of 300 million euros and whose lines of action will be the necessary equipment for programming and robotics in all the centers from children to secondary education, the specialized accompaniment of the teacher of children and primary in the teaching hours of programming, computational and robotic thinking and the elaboration of digital resources and teacher training. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, we are talking about general state budgets that raise a historical maximum in social spending and, therefore, also in education. In this context, we can be proud that education has preferential budgetary treatment, which corresponds to the needs of the country and the aspirations of the majority of our society. Our education system has improved a lot, mainly thanks to the work of the more than seven hundred thousand teachers and the continuous effort of the more than eight million students of our country, but we continue to have unalterable tasks to address in order to achieve this definitive educational convergence with the leading countries of Europe. In the last three years we will have invested more in education than in the last seven years of the previous stage, even without taking account of European funds or training for employment. That is the level of investment effort that our education needs and that must continue to achieve our goals as a country. In addition, the significant improvement in autonomous funding that all autonomous communities will have in the year 2023 will undoubtedly help to maintain this road map in favour of better education for all. That is why I ask you to support this draft budget for 2023. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]]
