ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u169	Thank you very much, President. Ladies and gentlemen, I appear before this House today to explain section 27 of the Draft Law on General State Budgets for 2023. Before I enter the digital field, I would like to share with your ladies and gentlemen a couple of thoughts on the debate and the vote that is to take place this week. And it is that these are fundamental budgets in order to be able to continue to deploy the investments of the recovery plan financed from the European funds Next Generation, in order to be able to continue to strengthen our welfare state and also to continue on the path of fiscal responsibility in a way compatible with economic growth and job creation; the path that we have already started last year. In these times so convulsed worldwide, marked by war, geopolitical tensions, turbulence in the energy and raw materials markets, the change in the sign of monetary policy and high uncertainty, it is more important than ever to have approved budgets in time and form, which send a clear message, giving certainty to international investors, to our partners, to international bodies, to companies and to citizens as a whole. And it is that political stability is an asset, and the Spanish Government has been building in these four years to have a positive balance today, with a coherent and balanced economic policy based on fiscal responsibility, social justice, progressive structural reforms; an economic policy that is well understood and supported by international agencies and markets. We say what we do and we do what we say. We approve the annual budgets in time and form. We meet our deficit and debt targets. We support strategic investments in our country. We acted effectively and decisively to solve the problems, trying to anticipate, as we did with the pandemic, and we are doing it again now, taking measures to contain the rise in prices, and yesterday to relieve families who may be most affected by the rise in mortgages. In short, Spain is at the moment a benchmark of stability and confidence in Europe, very important values in these times. In this context, all the estimates, all the agencies, coincide in the strong growth of the Spanish economy driven by the extraordinary behavior of the labour market, the good performance of the external sector and the dynamism of investment. Without going any further, yesterday the OECD revised the growth forecast for Spain up to 4.7% in 2022. [[Applauds]] 4.7%, above that of the Spanish Government itself. By 2023 the forecast is that the Spanish economy will continue to grow above the euro area average and its main partners. But, ladies and gentlemen, 4.7% is a strong growth of the Spanish economy, something that should give us confidence and satisfaction to all who want our country. The great uncertainty that we are experiencing today is leading to constant revisions of the forecasts, and that is why we have drawn up particularly prudent budgets, with a low income forecast, with a room for manoeuvre to respond to any scenario that may arise from the war in Ukraine, while continuing on the path of stability and fiscal consolidation. In this context, ladies and gentlemen, the importance of the annual approval of the general budgets of the State cannot be minimized, because this approval gives our country economic stability and our citizens social protection. Having general state budgets approved in time and form, approved and validated by the European Commission, is a very valuable intangible in economic policy. The other ministers have already spoken in detail about the record investment in scholarships for our students in Vocational Training - just mentioned in the previous debate - the increase in pensions, the freeness of public transport in the vicinity and the boost to sustainable mobility, the investments in the recovery plan to boost the green transition, and I will focus on the field of digital transformation, which is one of the fundamental axes for the modernization of our country at this time of intense technological change. As you know, our roadmap is part of the Digital Spain agenda, adopted in 2020 and updated in July of this year 2022, which has been definitely promoted thanks to the European funds Next Generation. We have already launched the main investment programmes in a triple area. First, infrastructure and technology, with flagship programs such as the SINGLE Program for Connectivity Infrastructures and 5G, artificial intelligence and cybersecurity strategies or the PERTE chip. Secondly, the digitisation of the economy with the Digital Kit for the digitisation of SMEs, the important investments in digitisation of the Administration, the impulse of Spain as an audiovisual hub of Europe and the PART of the new economy of the language. And, thirdly, people, with the various digital competence programs of citizens driven by the State, autonomous communities and local corporations, who increasingly reach out to citizens from all over the territory so that everyone can join digital change. This explains the significant increase in public investment in digital transformation, which will exceed by 2023 the figures, which have already been very significant, contained in the budget for 2022. In total, the investment of the general state budgets in digitisation in the period 2021-2023 multiplied by seven the investment made in 2018-2020. The ministry's overall budget amounts to more than EUR 6090 million by 2023, which represents an increase of 16.2% over 2022, and is that in 2023 the deployment of the recovery plan is accelerated, with an increase of more than 23% for the Next Generation funds, which are approaching the 49 billion euros of investment. Projects funded under the recovery plan are mostly investments in digitization. In addition to the chapters on digital transformation, I would like to point out two or three elements of the budget that may be interesting. The investments relating to the preparation of the Presidency of the Council of the European Union, which - as is known - Spain has in the second half of 2023, which amount to EUR 8 million in the case of the Ministry of Economy. A second point to note is that current transfers are down by 5%, mainly because they complete Greece's public debt relief contributions. Your ladies and gentlemen will recall that, in previous years, I pointed out this element of the budgets. Thirdly, the increase of EUR 30 million in the budget of 2023 for contributions to multilateral financial institutions, mainly linked to the fight against climate change, should be noted, although there is also an increase in the capital of the Latin American Development Bank, the CAF, to promote the integration and development programmes carried out by this institution. As I said, apart from these elements that I wanted to mention in passing, within the investments of these budgets for 2023, the most clearly important thing is investments in digitisation. Firstly, investments in PERTE Chip, the strategic project of semiconductors and microelectronics, which amount to EUR 1150 million in the budget for 2023. As you know, the objective of this strategic project is to strengthen the capabilities of our country's business ecosystem industry in this area, which is absolutely key to the strategic autonomy of Spain and Europe. This same Friday we will have a meeting with the ecosystem of companies, in the field of semiconductors and microelectronics, to open the second phase of the PART, once established the framework of action for its development and achieved the first advances, with the important investments already announced by Intel and Cisco, in the field of chip design. In addition to the investment for PERTE Chip, strong investments are provided in the budget for the deployment of the 5G connectivity plan and momentum, the SINGLE Programme mentioned above. The budgets of 2023 foresee more than EUR 1200 million to contribute to the objective of bringing ultra-fast broadband connectivity to 100% of the population by 2025, to maintain and strengthen Spain's leading position in terms of connectivity, that network that connects us, which is absolutely essential for the territorial vertebration of our country and the reduction of the digital gap between rural and urban environments. In addition, EUR 1340 million will be allocated in 2023 for the digitisation of small and medium-sized enterprises, mainly through the Digital Kit Programme. Another notable area is that of the data economy. We will allocate EUR 565 million for the creation of data spaces, especially in the field of tourism and health. The objective is precisely the digital transformation of this strategic tourism sector for our country, for the intensive use of big data and artificial intelligence, to improve the competitiveness of tourism companies and the development of new business models. And in the area of health, our goal is to create a health data centre that collects information from all systems and allows for a massive analysis for the identification and improvement of diagnosis and operation and the provision of service to all citizens, an absolutely key issue, as we have seen in recent years. We will continue to invest in the digital administration; we will dedicate more than 200 million euros in 2023, which join the 700 million we have already invested in the period 2021-2022, to develop a 21st century administration based on the provision of quality digital public services, close to citizens and able to boost the economy. We will devote more than EUR 135 million to cybersecurity, in order to further strengthen the capacities of citizens, businesses and professionals to respond to cyberattacks. The budgets of 2023 also include more than 40 million euros for the development of the digital competences of citizens. Since 2012, this programme has been endowed with almost EUR 11 billion, with the aim of improving citizens' basic digital skills; a subject that will undoubtedly have been addressed in detail by the Minister of Education, who has just participated in this Chamber. It completed the reference of the most important investments pointing to the strong commitment of the Government to women-led entrepreneurship. We will provide, in the budgets of 2023, 17 million euros for Enisa to continue to drive women-led startups. It is a line that we started in July 2021 and has already funded more than 100 projects led by women entrepreneurs in the digital field. All these figures, all these investments are not empty words, they are not declarations of intent, they are not a programme of the future, they are facts, they are investments on the ground that are being made here and now. We are in the process of digitisation and, thanks to the budgets of previous years, we have deployed important transformative investments in the lives of citizens and businesses, and on these investments we are going to build the budget execution of 2023. Let me give you a couple of examples. With the Unique Broadband Program, we are bringing the fastband, ultra-fast broadband, to more than 7100 municipalities across Spain. With the Digital Kit Program we have already provided digital solutions and tools to more than 52 000 SMEs. We have created Quantum Spain, the first quantum supercomputing ecosystem in southern Europe, in which the main supercomputing centers of several autonomous communities are participating and collaborating for this first quantum computer in southern Europe. In the field of artificial intelligence, we are facilitating the integration of this new technology into industrial value chains, helping 210 leading companies in the sector in the first call, 70% of which are SMEs. We are enhancing the use of Spanish and co-official languages in the technological field, so that artificial intelligence not only thinks in Chinese and English, but also in Spanish, Spanish and co-official languages. Recently, we have approved the programme of support for projects developed in the autonomous communities: The AINA project, for Catalan; GAITU, for Basque; US, for Galician; VIVES, for Valencian; and LEIA for Spanish, with the Royal Academy. In digital competitions, we have just launched the Digital Pymes Generation Program. We have launched the call to train 95 000 SME managers and 33 000 agents of change, to strengthen the digital transformation of the economy, in addition to the programmes that are being launched by the autonomous communities and the municipalities for the digital competences of the whole citizenry and all the digital competence programmes at school and at the VET, of which the Minister of Education has already spoken in detail, and all this, guided by a perspective of humanistic digitalisation that puts the person at the centre. The Digital Rights Charter, which we adopted in July 2021, gives us a frame of reference for all developments, all the programmes that are put in place, and ensure that our democratic values, rights, are protected equally in the physical world and in the online world. Thanks to this momentum, and the results are tangible, according to the latest report of the European Commission, DESI 2022, the index of the digital economy and society, Spain has taken the seventh position of the 27 Member States of the European Union in digitisation. [[Applauds]] To give you an idea, when we arrived at the government, in 2018, Spain held the eleventh position in this index. Spain has been consolidated as the most advanced country among the great, ahead of France, Italy and Germany, and is also ahead of countries traditionally emblematic of digital transformation, such as Estonia or Portugal. That is, that these investments, this digital agenda is already having results on the ground and is demonstrating Spain's ability to lead in the digital sphere, in this technological revolution that, for the first time in our history, finds us well prepared, with strong infrastructures, with the recovery plan to make the necessary investments and with a government absolutely determined to take advantage of all opportunities without leaving anyone behind. These general state budgets for 2023 in this context are more important than ever. We are at a historic moment and the decisions we now make will mark the future of our country. They are good budgets for Spain and its citizens, so that they can have an economic and social transformation and move towards a greener, more digital, fairer, more equitable, better vertebrate and more cohesive from a social and territorial point of view; and all this, with full fiscal responsibility, as I said a moment ago, fulfilling our objectives to further reduce the deficit and debt ratios on GDP, as we have been doing in recent years. The policy budget for the eradication of machist violence amounts to EUR 320 million, allocating 56% of the total budget for this purpose. These budgets will enable us to improve the capacity for prevention and early detection of violence against women, open crisis centres for 24 hours so that any victim of sexual violence has close, and without need of reporting, the best specialized psychological care, legal advice, social workers who accompany them in access to their economic and social rights, improve the 016 service and ensure the proper working conditions of their staff, promote the activity of the feminist associative fabric, which saves lives, ladies and gentlemen, or modernize the tools available for the protection of women victims of any form of macho violence. These budgets will allow us to deploy the 'yes only' law, a law that guarantees for the first time in Spain a framework for effective and robust protection of the rights of women victims of sexual violence to prevent and detect such violence early, to provide all women with comprehensive, specialized and also reparation care. [[applause]] These budgets allow us to make progress in the implementation of the State Pact against Gender Violence and improve its funding. The investment of public resources, together with the reference catalogue of policies and services on violence that we have just adopted at a sectoral conference, will enable us to improve the quality and scope of specialized public services throughout the territory for victims of gender-based violence. I would also like to focus on public investment in the Correspondent Plan, the first public policy of conciliation articulated by the State in coordination with the autonomous communities and the municipalities, an essential pillar in order to advance the guarantee of the right to care in our country. This Corresponding Plan, which we launched in the year 2021 with 190 million euros and which continued with a second heading of 200 million in 2022, is consolidated in this new budget year with a heading of 201 million euros. This plan translates into essential public policies for the day-to-day lives of many families, particularly of many women: ludotheques and public childcare spaces that allow reconciliation outside school hours, professionals who provide support on school and out-of-school routes and transfers, reinforcement of school canteens and camps or home care for children aged 0 to 3 years. I remind you that, according to data from the International Labour Organization, approximately 3.8 million people in Spain are engaged in paid care work. The Corresponsible Plan also dignifies and recognizes this work, a feminized work, an invisible work, a precarious work, but without which, as we have heard in so many feminist mobilizations, it is for the world. With these budgets, we therefore recognize that care must not only be distributed more equitably between women and men, but also that the State must be co-responsible. These budgets also send a message to women and to society as a whole. It is precisely in times of difficulty that we must make the most progress on the rights of more than half of the population that we are women. We are not complacent and know that much remains to be done, but we are determined to move forward. For the crises the right proposes corruption, privileges for the richest and cuts for the social majority, to carry the worst consequences of the crisis on the bodies of women who care for their homes and their families, who occupy the most precarious jobs, who suffer the consequences of cuts and privatizations in health, education or dependency. With the economic management of the right, women and working people are being cut back on their rights as they see the money of everyone being handed over to the big financial companies without asking them to give it back, and the rich and corrupt benefit from their tax amnesties. The right-wing side believes that good economic management is about increasing inequalities, particularly gender inequalities. The feminist majority of this Congress and the coalition government know that economic management is good when it is possible to deal with inequality, especially at the most difficult times. Increasing public investment in feminist policies, i.e., in public conciliation services, in dependency policies, in services that protect women who are victims of sexist violence, is a necessary condition to deal with difficulties from the ordinary and without making them fall on women's bodies. I'll tell you this with figures. The investment of these general state budgets in feminist policies is almost seven times greater, ladies and gentlemen, seven times greater than the resources allocated by Mr M's People's Party. Rajoy from the papers of Bárcenas to feminist rights. In the worst years of the financial crisis, with Mr Moreno Bonilla at the head of the Secretariat of State for Equality, there was a cut of 46% of the budget, which was 41 million euros against the current 573. No wonder you don't want to talk about feminist budgets, ladies and gentlemen, because these data are valued alone. [[Applauds]] Finally, I would like to make a brief reference to the gender impact budget memory. Of the 379 expenditure programmes, 372, 98% were considered gender sensitive. Of these, 20 % are of high relevance. This means that in this budget year we allocate more than €57 billion with high relevance to gender impact, that is, they make a decisive contribution to progress towards equality between women and men. We are talking about public policies of employment, education, entrepreneurship, fiscal policies, policies aimed at the family or at children's rights that directly impact in their development and implementation on the improvement of the living conditions of all women in our country and particularly those who need it most. Thanks - and I conclude - to all the parliamentary groups, particularly those of the feminist, progressive and plurinational majority of investiture, which are striving to improve these budgets and to make this important public policy tool a tool for the women of our State. Thanks to the feminist majority in this House for committing to the advances in feminist rights despite the fierce resistance we are encountering and we will find ourselves on the way. All of them should know that every time they come they will find us working and winning rights. Let women's struggle live! Thank you very much. ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u244	Thank you so much. We move on to the debate in section 31, Ministry of Consumer Affairs. For the section presentation, I have the floor on behalf of the Government, the Minister for Consumer Affairs. [[Rumors]] Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u245	Thank you very much, President. Good afternoon, everyone and everyone. It is a pleasure to be able to present here the programme and budget lines of the ministry, in this case, for the third consecutive year; a year in which the budget lines grow and, therefore, increase the resources and the possibility of carrying out a series of programmes that will meet the objectives that we have set at the beginning of this parliamentary term and in each of the present challenges. This is the increase in resources of 11.38 % in the Ministry's budgets; resources will be used to further strengthen and support the commitments recorded in the coalition agreement, with the credibility that we have in the Ministry of having already fulfilled 70 % of the commitments recorded and reflected in that coalition agreement. Therefore, in this ministry we are fulfilling practically day by day with the planned programming and, therefore, meeting the objectives that we set ourselves at the beginning of the legislature. I will sum it up in three blocks, which I believe represent very well the spirit of the policies that we are carrying out in the ministry and from the government, and which have to do, firstly, with healthy consumption, with food security, nutrition and ecological impact; secondly, with gambling and gambling, and thirdly, with the rights of consumers. The context you know perfectly well, ladies and gentlemen, is a context of crisis, in the sense that the invasion of the territory of Ukraine by Russia, combined with the effects produced after that invasion, plus what still remains, unfortunately, of pandemics and other phenomena, are added to the structural situation of a climate change that is altering economic relations, among other things, throughout the world. This is causing a series of deep problems throughout the global supply network, and obviously in the supply network, which reaches the last link in the chain that consumers are. The shortage and increase of fossil and mineral resources has been transferred to consumer goods and food in the form of scarcity and rising prices, especially losing the usual ones, the most humble families, the working families. It is important that we remember that, even if war were to be stopped immediately, and even if the flows of raw materials, minerals and energy sources such as fossil fuels were to be incorporated again, we would still have a very serious problem in this regard, which has to do with the time of the abundance of cheap energy being over. Therefore, we have to adapt our societies not only to the obvious phenomenon of climate change, but also to the reality that we are built societies, from infrastructure to probably the worldview we have in our heads, to the heat of fossil fuels and, therefore, we are particularly vulnerable to the dynamics of this market. This is what is called an ecological transition and it is what this Government is committed to, as it has been defended on many other occasions. We talk not only about climate change but about biodiversity loss, we talk about food systems. Therefore, we talk about safe food, we talk about how to ensure that our population can be fed within the limits of the planet. Therefore, the objective of this ministry from the beginning has been to broaden the horizons of action of public policies, incorporating this reflection and incorporating it into their public policies, as I will explain below. It is about putting the consumer as a protagonist and not simply as a mere recipient of the ecological transformation we need. That is why the first block has to do with the Spanish Food Safety and Nutrition Agency, which, as you know, is a fundamental pillar in everything about food. It is an agency that emerged at its origin with the aim of fulfilling everything related to food security, which was later extended by different governments of different signs also to the area of nutrition and, Currently, we are also incorporating that related to the ecological impact that our food has. Therefore, three aspects: food safety, nutrition and ecology or ecological impact, which are closely interrelated. The agency is increasing its budgets this year by 7.19 % for this year, which is in fact more than 21 % compared to the start of the legislature. This not only improves food security capacities but in the rest of the areas, as I have just described. We continue to deepen with the agency in the line of work on healthy and sustainable diets because, we remember, we have rates of overweight and obesity, particularly infantile, absolutely intolerable, of 40% among children between the ages of six and nine and for those for whom, as for the whole population, a better diet also means better health. We have the fortune that moving our diet towards a healthy diet is also making a more sustainable diet. In Spain we have the advantage of having a Mediterranean diet, which is unfortunately being lost to the benefit of the consumption of ultra-processed products and products that ultimately harm the individual health and the health of the planet. It is a matter of recovering these diets much more intense in products of plant origin than in products of animal origin, such as the Mediterranean diet, as well as recovering models of agro-food production that favor the consumption of proximity and ways of consuming that keep us away from the possibility of reproducing new zoonotic diseases, such as COVID. With this budget, AESAN reinforces this line of work, as it also reinforces its work of surveillance, control, strengthening, for example, the National Food Centre and the Biotoxin Reference Laboratory Marinas de Vigo, which I had the opportunity to visit a very short time ago to check how the use of technology and modernization is fundamental to ensuring food security. New campaigns to promote the consumption of healthy and sustainable diets, along the lines outlined above, will also be promoted with this budget. We will work, as we are doing, with the actual decree of school menus and menus in senior residences and, of course, promote proximity consumption and local consumption, in line with what we have been doing in the last three years. In addition, we will have in these budgets the study ALADINO 2023, from which we obtained the figure that I have just given of obesity index and overweight. For the next year, the effects of the pandemic on eating habits and, in particular, its effects on health will be incorporated for the first time, most likely very asymmetrically in social classes. The second block refers to gambling and gambling. At the moment - probably still playing the game of the Spanish selection - is in progress the world wide. This is a very sensitive time for those people who are suffering the consequences of compulsive gambling, who have had some kind of gambling disorder. These are moments of great sensitivity, when sporting competitions are produced in a fairly important way, all kinds of bets, as has happened in previous sporting competitions phenomena, for example, the last Euro Cup. Fortune, which is not the result of chance but in this case of the work of this Government, has already brought into force in our country the real decree of advertising gambling and gambling, which restricts advertising and therefore makes it not in our country at this time that invasion and bombardment of advertising gambling and gambling that was typical of the times, such as the current one, of sports competitions. In fact, Spain is the only country in the European Union where this regulation is now in place and it is the only country that has a certain degree of tranquillity at the moment, unlike the other countries that are currently experiencing an important and sensitive situation with regard to the invasion of advertising and the vulnerability of a part of the population particularly sensitive to this matter. The Directorate-General for Gambling will strengthen with these budgets the inspection capacity and will facilitate mechanisms with the interconnection of self-prohibited registers which, as you know - because we have explained it on previous occasions - are the tools used by the autonomous communities so that those who consider that they do not have sufficient will, or who want to guarantee a self-prohibition in some gambling or gambling hall, can register in that register of the autonomous community to have prohibited and vetoed access to those premises. However, the lack of interconnection makes it possible to move at times of enormous sensitivity to another autonomous community where such a register is invalid. What we are doing with this budget is equipping us with the possibility of interconnecting those registers and, therefore, increasing the protection of people in vulnerable situations; a budget which also serves to intensify the fight against fraud in sports betting and illegal gambling, and which will also allow us to deepen - we hope - the legislative proposal of the law against so-called booty boxes, which are like gambling within the video games themselves. We also take advantage of the possibility of reorienting the fee for administrative gambling management paid by operators through their gross operating income, which results in an improvement in the protection of consumers of this type of activity and which allows us to draw up certain programme lines in particular; for example, a mechanism for the detection of risk-behavioural players, which has been used with operators, to which more than 800,000 euros have been allocated; grants which have a fairly obvious purpose, for example, subsidies to reference hospitals of the National Health System for the treatment of those addictive behaviors or behaviors, with 100,000 euros, or grants to associations and federations of associations of exjuices and rehabilitated players to obtain data that allow the development of the algorithm of problematic behavior of the game, also with more than 100,000 euros. It is also a matter of repeating with this budget the promotion of research projects focusing on the effects and risks of gambling activities, as well as deepening awareness and prevention campaigns, focusing on people in vulnerable situations in the face of possible risk behaviours. Let us remember that we have managed to close the vector by which people enter into this compulsive activity involving risks, despite being a deep and totally legal activity. We have achieved this with advertising regulation, but there are still a large number of players, there is still a face-to-face game and there is still a problem that we have to continue to solve. Finally, in the third block, we have everything to say about the protection of consumers. In this case, we see an increase of 17% more, therefore 27% since the start of the legislature. This percentage is high, above all, thanks to the fact that we have finally incorporated in the capacity of the State the creation and development of sanctioning powers in the face of major fraud and abuse of consumption. We have claimed this and it has been a long process, because, as you know, there was no such capacity, and therefore many behaviours of large companies linked to fraud and abuse were, as is popularly said, rositas because there was no regulation or sanctioning capacity on the part of the State and what existed in the autonomous communities was insufficient and in some cases failed to meet its objectives. Thanks to that royal decree that we were able to launch, which was validated in this House, we already have that sanctioning capacity, which now, in this budget, is equipped with the personnel necessary to put an end to an obvious democratic deficit, and that is that when a large company commits an abuse or fraud it must be penalized. This, even if it was common sense, did not happen in our country until now. We will also invest in the digitisation and renewal of the equipment and facilities and laboratories of the Directorate-General for Consumer Affairs, such as the Centre for Research and Quality Control; we will promote and strengthen the Internet Consumer Observatory, the European Consumer Centre, and a number of other work lines that will be strengthened by this budget, as well as promote regulatory development on universal accessibility in labelling. Just as in the two previous aspects or blocks in which I have structured this intervention, we will continue to promote awareness, awareness and information of consumers so that they know their rights. It's over now. In short, ladies and gentlemen, I have tried to summarise very briefly some of the central issues. Of course, my presentation does not exhaust all the lines of work collected in the ministry, let alone in these budgets, but they are lines of work of the Government of Spain in sensitive and strategic matters. It's about food security, what we all eat to feed ourselves, to get energy in the day-to-day. This is public, animal and human health, within the One Health paradigm of the World Health Organization, assumed by the Spanish Food and Nutrition Security Agency and the Ministry of Consumer Affairs. We talk about the protection of children in all matters of consumption, such as the consumption of toys; we talk about the protection of the most vulnerable; we talk about the improvement of eating habits, which are improvements in our health and in the health of the planet; we talk about the support to those who fight and fight the ludopathy and other disorders of the game and the causes that generate it; we talk about the defense against the frauds and abuses of large companies or the awareness and action against the climate crisis and the eco-social crisis that our society is going through and that is therefore a fundamental pillar of the activity of this ministry. I therefore ask you, as in the other sections, to vote in favour so that these budgets can continue to develop an absolutely necessary line of work for the ministry, for the Government of Spain and for the democratic society we represent. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u256	Thank you very much, Mrs Sánchez. We move on to the debate in sections 32, Ministry of Social Security and Migration Inclusion, and 60, Social Security. For the presentation of the sections, the Minister for Inclusion, Social Security and Migration has the floor on behalf of the Government. I'll ask for some silence first, please. [[Pause]]
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u257	President, ladies and gentlemen, before I begin my speech, allow me to express my absolute solidarity with my Government colleague, Irene Montero, who has received some absolutely unusual disqualifications a few minutes ago in this House. [[Applauds]] I compare with you to report on the general state budgets of the Ministry of Inclusion, Social Security and Migration for 2023. These are very relevant budgets not only because they show progress in our reform commitments in the various areas of the ministry, but also because they respond to a particularly complex international context. Our main objective is to mitigate the effects of the inflationary shock resulting from the conflict in Ukraine, limiting its impact on citizenship and, above all, giving certainty to the vulnerable. And in this context, there are three main elements on which our budget revolves. Firstly, with these budgets in 2023, one in five people in this country will retain their purchasing power as a result of the revaluation of pensions and of the minimum living income, and at the same time we will move towards the budgetary balance of Social Security, so that the social protection and sustainability of the system are simultaneously strengthened with accounts whose solidity is supported by the continued increase in employment. Secondly, we want to consolidate the inclusion policies that will enable progress in poverty reduction, and the inclusion policies that were launched in June 2020 with the approval in this House of minimum living income accompany and reinforce this benefit in order to ensure that the most vulnerable can be integrated into our society. Thirdly, we will continue to improve the refugee reception model, enhancing regular, orderly and secure migration, as well as the care of displaced persons from the conflict in Ukraine. And along with these three elements, there are two factors that are cross-cutting in our action: the modernization of the ministry, linked to the Transformation and Resilience Recovery Plan, and the strengthening of human capital, especially diminished after ten years of continuous reduction of Social Security personnel, while, on the other hand, it has grown its role in protecting citizens in order to respond to the very adverse situations that we are having to face in this legislature. Of the three areas of management entrusted to this ministry, I begin with Social Security, whose departure is extraordinarily relevant. In quantitative terms, it represents one third of the budget of all public administrations, and qualitatively constitutes a central pillar of our welfare state. In this area, we have already developed most of the recommendations that you, ladies and gentlemen, made to us before the Committee on the Toledo Pact - seventeen of twenty-one recommendations - and we have dealt with them as you have instructed us, with an intense social dialogue and by building broad agreements and consensuses, which are the best guarantee of long-term policy stability. In addition, I remind you that many of these recommendations are milestones in the recovery plan in its component 30, referring to the sustainability of pensions, which we have been scrupulously fulfilling, according to the demanding timetable that we have set ourselves. The implementation of the amendment introduced by the Law on Guaranteeing the Purchasing Power of Pensions and other measures to strengthen the financial and social sustainability of our public pension system is undoubtedly the most important aspect of this budget. [[Rumors]]
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u258	Quiet, please.
ParlaMint-ES_2022-11-23-CD221123.u259	We comply with the right to the revaluation of pensions under the CPI, which we estimate will increase for all pensions by around 8.5% in 2023. Another central element of the budget has to do with the new system of contributions for net returns for self-employed workers resulting from the important agreement in the framework of the social dialogue and with the main self-employed organisations which was validated by this House at the end of August. The new system of autonomous contributions is more flexible and equitable and will provide greater protection for this group. Its implementation will be done gradually, with a temporary deficiency that will allow its revision within three years and with a final horizon in 2032, when we will already have the system fully deployed. We are responding to a historical demand from the self-governing collective, which had previously been attempted with different governments, without any result. On the other hand, the amendment of four articles of the General Law on Social Security and the ongoing process of changing a regulation of 1992 will enable the real estate assets of Social Security to be managed more flexibly, providing with due guarantees and in certain circumstances that their unused assets can be used by other public administrations to fulfil their social objectives. And continuing with the implementation of the reforms already adopted, I would like to point out that, in compliance with the first recommendation of the Toledo Pact on so-called inappropriate expenditure, these budgets incorporate a contribution from the State that is close to EUR 20 billion. It is important to recall at this point the development of the Social Security Reserve Fund. If non-contributory expenditure by the General Administration of the State had been financed separately since 2011, the significant reduction in the Reserve Fund deposits, which had virtually exhausted it, would have been avoided. In these budgets of 2023 we recover the contributions to the Reserve Fund, which we promote with about 28 billion euros, linked mainly to the new mechanism of intergenerational equity. This opens a path of successive allocations to the Reserve Fund for the coming years. It is therefore, and I would like to highlight, the first time since 2011 that the provision of the Reserve Fund has been increased. This is a real turning point, which will allow us to be in the best position to cope with the transitional situation that will entail the retirement of a particularly large generation. In addition, I would particularly like to highlight the dynamism of social security income. Growth is already extraordinary in 2022. Social contributions reach eleven points of GDP, one and a half points more than the average between 2013 and 2019. This good behaviour will be maintained in 2023, with a growth forecast of 8.4%. We have said this many times: The best guarantee of the sustainability of our social protection system is to have a dynamic and resilient labour market that boosts quality employment. Ladies and gentlemen, the social security data in terms of membership and collection are indisputable in this sense month after month. Despite the uncertainties of the macroeconomic context, our labour market is undergoing an extraordinary transformation and contributes to the structural strengthening of social security income. On these dates we can already estimate that the average growth of social security membership, employment at the end of the day, will be in 2022 at around 4%, an exceptional growth with any historical reference we take. Since the recovery of the pre-pandemic level in August 2021, more than 825 000 jobs have been created, of which some 500 000 will be created this year. These are extraordinary figures, which reflect a much greater dynamism in employment than in comparable countries in our European environment, some of which have not even reached the level of pre-pandemic employment. The improvement in the quality of employment, as a result mainly of labour reform, is also evident in the growth of two-digit occupations in high productivity sectors, as well as in the greater stability of employment. It highlights the significant reduction in temporality, especially among children under the age of 30. Today, three out of four young people have an indefinite job, compared to one out of two before the labour reform. Ladies and gentlemen, this good development in social contributions is going to help make the social security deficit half the GDP by 2023, which means maintaining the level of 2022 after making all the revaluation of pensions. This result is very positive, but it is even more positive if we take into account that in 2017 the social security deficit was 1.6% of GDP, three times higher. We all have to congratulate ourselves on this outcome. It consolidates a clear path towards budgetary balance despite the enormous difficulties of the situation and the effort we are going to make to improve the package of benefits. The budget therefore reflects the solidity of the Social Security system, a solidity that will be reinforced by the full deployment of the measures already taken and those that we are going to put in place for the implementation of the mandates of the Toledo Pact and the government's commitments to the European authorities. We therefore approach the future with confidence, giving certainty to our present and future pensioners and strengthening the social and financial sustainability of the system. Secondly, I would like to refer to the budget for inclusion policies for 2023, a budget that consolidates the objective of reducing social inequalities and poverty and ensuring income coverage for the most vulnerable. This completes the development of inclusion policies, as we progressed when in June 2020 we approved minimum living income in this House. At the beginning of this legislature, Spain was a country with a very unequal distribution of income among households. Before the arrival of the minimum income, the minimum income benefits, which had been developed in a heterogeneous manner in the autonomous communities, offered an overall result that generated inequities. The introduction of minimum living income has corrected this situation by establishing a country-wide protection floor to protect the most vulnerable. Moreover, the economic situation in recent years has shown that there is no sustainable growth in the medium term if growth is not inclusive. Economic efficiency and social equity must be complementary, and they are. The design of vital minimum income was never a benefit, but a set of policies that allow us to have a last social protection network, contributing to the goal of equity for which we all work. In just two and a half years, minimum living income has helped more than 530,000 households and one and a half million beneficiaries. Of these, one in five are single-parent households, two in three beneficiaries are women and more than 40% are children, thanks to the addition of the child support supplement, which already reaches 285 000 families, who receive between 50 and 100 euros per child. In parallel, we know that these benefits have potential beneficiaries who do not request it, and we are making every effort to narrow this coverage gap. Therefore, the ministry is focusing all efforts on maximizing protection thresholds, and various measures have been deployed to this end. For the first time in Spain, the municipalities are already certifying situations of social vulnerability, in addition to having eleven mediators through non-governmental organizations, which also allow us to obtain such certification. We have made direct contact from the ministry with 90,000 potential beneficiaries via SMS messages and we are enabling a mobile office for information and processing of minimum vital income, the minimum vital income bus to proactively direct all these people. I would like to stress, ladies and gentlemen, that the provision, the minimum living income, is not an end in itself, but a tool to facilitate the transition of individuals from social exclusion, which imposes the absence of resources on them to a situation in which they can develop fully in society. That is why we are working on the development of thirty-four inclusion routes throughout the national territory, financed with 212 million of the recovery plan and with a methodology that can measure the impact of social policy. We are working with the autonomous communities, municipalities and entities of the third sector, in addition to the advice of external experts and evaluators. We will begin to see results in 2023 and, according to them, we will be able to scale up the best measures, which will be most effective in promoting social and labour inclusion policies among the most vulnerable people. In this regard, we have recently approved the employment incentive, which will come into force next year, and to those who find employment or increase the number of hours worked, if they already have, will allow them to have more disposable income than if they did not, encouraging them to participate in the labour market to help them get out of the poverty situation. We are developing the social inclusion label, with which we involve companies in the inclusion of recipients of minimum living income publicly recognizing their dedication. In conclusion, minimum living income is a substantial part of the social agenda of the Government of Spain to reduce poverty and enhance social inclusion, a policy that represents a great step forward in the welfare state of our country. Finally, I will refer to migration policy. The 2023 budget will allow us to continue to develop a set of policies that are substantially transforming our migration model for the first time in many years. The first axis of the change is the new model of the reception system to provide it with stability and efficiency and will enter into force in 2023. In this reception system, in which there are now more than 35 000 people, the current model of grants to collaborating entities will be replaced by a concert model procedure open to all those who wish to participate, in which stability and long-term vision prevails through four-year planning. Thus, we will be able to have defined and expandable reception capacities and facilitate closer collaboration between the Administration and the entities, in a framework of higher legal certainty through continuous exchange of information and greater evaluation and transparency. To this end, 215 million of the recovery plan are being invested to finance the creation of a real public ownership network, which will allow to move from 400 places in 2019 to 6100 places in 2024. Unlike what we find when we arrive at the Government, this investment will enable us to have a reception system to deal with the migration crises that will enable us to address the migration reality of our country. In parallel, we will continue to welcome and assist the 150 000 Ukrainians who have so far applied for temporary protection in our country. For this we have opened in record time four reception centres, the CREADE, and we are financing all expenditure on education, health and social services, developing a system of aid and addressing the social and labour integration of the displaced, so that there are already more than 13 000 Ukrainians working in Spain. The second axis of the change in the migration model is the deployment of the reform of the regulations of the Aliens Act to ensure orderly, regular and safe migration, reducing irregular migration routes, which entail high social, human, economic and management costs, dignified the lives of migrants and ensuring that migration policy complements other policies to meet the social and economic needs of our country. That is why we are adopting the best practices of the countries in our environment and supporting national activity and employment. We know that in order for this reform to be possible, an effort is needed in the model of foreign management, reducing administrative burdens and simplifying procedures. To this end we will be fully developing a new management unit, the UTEX, in 2023, which through centralized management will make this transformation possible. Finally, I would like to stress that, together with the change in the Spanish migration model that we are carrying out, a sustainable European model is needed, which will enable us to face crises such as that of Ukraine and respond to competition for talent at international level. During the year 2023 the Spanish Presidency in the European Union will be an opportunity to continue working towards a much needed European migration and asylum pact. Ladies and gentlemen, as you have heard, the budget for 2023 in this ministry is aimed at strengthening our welfare model by maintaining the purchasing power, especially of the most vulnerable, by providing security and certainty to pensioners present and future by eliminating uncertainties and reinforcing the sustainability of the pension system, by completing social inclusion policies, which make sustainable growth possible, and by undertaking a definitive reform of the migration model, by stabilising the host model, by deigning migrants and moving towards regular, orderly and secure migration. They are all structural reforms that we are addressing with determination and ambition, equipping them with the elements that make them sustainable policies over time, responding to commitments made at the beginning of the legislature and strengthening our coexistence model and our welfare state. Thank you very much. [[Applauds]]

The Ministry's budget, without autonomous bodies, for the next fiscal year amounts to a total of €441.65 million, including in this amount almost 130 million of the PRTR, the Recovery, Transformation and Resilience Plan. The above represents a clear budgetary increase of more than 10%, without taking into account the funds of the recovery plan that have been distributed in a decreasing way over the last few years in order to generate more rapid resource generation by universities. Since 2020, the budget for universities has been increased by almost 80%. This increase has been due to the PRTR, but also to the other budgetary programmes which have increased by more than 25% in these three years, thus changing the downward path of budgeting and funding of the university system that had been characterized since 2009. Looking at the distribution of the budget in the various budget chapters, he would also highlight a number of elements. For example, almost EUR 20 million, or 4.45% of the budget, is earmarked for Chapter 1. Current expenditure on goods and services amounts to €7.7 million. Current transfers amount to EUR 148 million, which is a third of the budget. Capital transfers reach EUR 264 million, almost 60% of the budget. This distribution of the budget between the various budget chapters follows the same patterns as in previous years, and is as follows: The vast majority of the budget, i.e. around 94 %, goes directly to programmes with a direct impact on the university system and, consequently, on citizenship, while only the remaining 6 % goes to the Ministry's operations, 4.45 to staff costs and 1.75% to current expenditure on goods and services. Given these overall figures of the budget and its distribution by chapters, I began to analyse the various spending programmes of the ministry. In education policy we have three programmes. Program 322.C, dedicated to university education. The appropriations for this programme are increased by EUR 6.20 million compared to the budget for 2022. Here, for example, are the transfers to UNED - which, as you know, is a unique university that depends directly on the ministry and not on the autonomous communities, such as the rest of the cases - and the programme of European universities. It would highlight several aspects, for example, the consolidation of a budget of EUR 3.5 million to provide national funding to both public and private universities participating in the programme of European universities, in which the presence of Spain is very relevant, as I said earlier, with more than thirty universities present, being above countries such as Italy and being at the same level as countries with more populations such as Germany or France. There is an increase of EUR 3 million due to the organisation of events in the framework of the Spanish Presidency of the Council of the European Union, where we will have for the first time a meeting of all the directors who are part of the network of European universities that I have just mentioned. There is also an increase in transfers to UNED of more than €800,000. A new item that I would particularly like to highlight is the one dedicated to promoting for the first time in Spain the citizen science, with a budget of 400,000 euros and an increase of 200,000 euros in the transfer that the University of Granada receives to finance the campuses of Ceuta and Melilla. The second programme of education policy is 323.M, dedicated to scholarships and student grants. This programme incorporates, for example, the national end-of-care awards, the scholarships awarded by the IUMP - Menéndez Pelayo International University - for assistance in their economic activities or the national co-financing of the Erasmus+ programme, which manages the Spanish Service for the Internationalization of Education, SEPIE. It is also worth noting an increase of €70,000 over the previous year, which is mainly due to the increase in the allocation of the Campus Inclusive programme. The last programme of this policy, 321.O, is of an instrumental nature, since its objective is to make possible the implementation of the other programmes mentioned above. Within this programme, there has also been an increase of EUR 300000 in current expenditure on goods and services, mainly due to the increase in costs expected for both raw materials and the planned expenditure for the Spanish Presidency of the Council of the European Union in the second half of 2023. With regard to research, development, innovation and digitisation policy programmes, the budget includes three programmes. The programme 463.A, on scientific research, is experiencing significant growth, 18 million euros, representing more than 15%, to cover several topics, for example, aid for the training of university professors - FPU -, short stays, temporary transfers and FPU linked to the European University Institute of Florence. I would like to stress that the increase in the FPU grants is 18 million euros, reaching a total of 98,865,000 euros. In addition, there is an increase in contract aid from 885 to 900, with an improvement in wage growth, because it has been established that the wage fixed in the EPIF will increase by 60% in the second year and that it will reach 75% of the wage fixed in the EPIF in the third and fourth years. Increase in the number of aids reserved for persons with disabilities up to 5%. At the same time, an amount corresponding to the award for the end of the contract has been incorporated, because these scholarships for years already have a contract logic. The Beatriz Galindo grants, for the attraction of the research talent to the universities, previews a departure of 14 million euros. [[Rumors]]

Having done this review and before giving way to final conclusions, allow me to refer to some normative forecasts that are also included in the project and that we believe are of great importance to the university system. On the one hand, and in the line of strengthening the teaching and research capacity of universities, the budget for the year 2023 once again considers that teaching and research staff and administrative and service staff are a priority sector from the perspective of public supply. This means that the replacement rate for both groups will be 120 per cent next year. Thus, the replacement rate for this year, 2022, which increased by 10% - it was 110 - is maintained for the year 2023, reaching 120 %. This is quite important because it allows the renovation of all the staff who, as you well know, in some universities have a remarkable aging rate. Just as this year, public universities in the same community are allowed to transfer replacement rates with the authorization of the Autonomous Government, which may be relevant in the case of universities of different ages, for example the University of Seville and the UPO, which are very different in old age and can share this joint replacement rate instead. However, the Government's strong will to promote the rejuvenation of university staff and to reverse a situation arising from the policies of previous Governments that jeopardized the very sustainability of the university system is highlighted. I'm done. Let me reiterate that the draft budgets for the 2023 financial year take a step further in the commitment of the Government and this ministry to the access and quality of the Spanish university system, as well as to its contribution to the transformation of our productive model. The draft budget not only clearly increases the economic resources allocated to the university system, but also structures and contains budget forecasts designed to make the Spanish university more accessible, to improve the quality of the university system through the training, qualification and rejuvenation of its faculty and to modernise universities in areas as important as digitisation and internationalisation. Ladies and gentlemen, I urge you once again, please, to reflect, to reflect on the image we have taken outside this House individually and collectively. We are the Parliament of Spain, we are the representatives of all the citizens of this country, ladies and gentlemen, and if we want a society that is respected, we must respect each other. I ask you, please, that there should be no more incidents like the ones we have experienced today. Thank you so much. [[Applauds]] We'll vote in three minutes.
